研究員/王建智 MICHAELWANG82121188@UNI-
PSG.COM

研究部對於長榮看法轉趨正向，係因：(1) 3Q26 受惠貨櫃航運
旺季及運價回升，平均運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅， 帶動獲
利顯著成長。(2) 4Q26 雖進入傳統淡季，惟北美農曆春節前備
貨 、歐洲貨量成長及亞洲區域航線旺季提供支撐，預期獲利雖低
於 3Q26，但仍優於 2Q26。(3) 2027 年雖面臨新船交付增加的供
給壓力， 但全球碼頭擴建速度落後船隊成長，加上塞港及紅海繞
航持續消耗有效運力，有助減緩新增供給對運價的衝擊， 北美及
歐洲航線長約運價亦預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利。預估
2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為45.21 元（+42.7%YoY）／27.89 元
（-38.3%YoY）。歷史本淨比區間為 0.4～1.8 倍，以 2027 年底
預估每股淨值 298.36 元為評價基礎，給予0.95 倍本淨比， 目標
價 283 元，將投資評等調升至買進。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 運價漲幅高於燃油成本增幅，帶動獲利顯著成長 
長榮 3Q26 營收 1,467.76 億元，+39.6%QoQ/+51.4%YoY，主要受
惠第三季貨櫃航運傳統旺季， 歐美航線進入零售商備貨高峰， 帶
動貨運需求回升，加上紅海繞航、 全球港口壅塞持續消耗有效運
力，以及航商透過艙位控管調節供給，推升主要航線運價。7、8
月平均運費分別為 1,539 及1,577 美元／TEU， 參考9 月SCFI 月
均 3,657 點，+8.0%MoM， 推估9 月平均運費約 1,703 美元／TEU，
並假設 9 月貨量與 8 月持平，則 3Q26 平均運費約 1,606 美元／
TEU，+44.7%QoQ，貨量276 萬 TEU，-1.8%QoQ，營收成長主要由
運價上漲貢獻。

成本方面 ， 預估每噸耗油成本由2Q26 的 645 美元升至 714 美元 ，
+10.6%QoQ，假設季度耗油量持平，燃油費用增加約22 億元，惟
運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅 ， 營收增加帶來的獲利貢獻可望
大幅超過成本上升影響。預估 3Q26 稅後淨利 479.69 億元，
+199.2%QoQ/+120.6%YoY，稅後EPS 22.16 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

圖一、各月平均運費、每噸耗油成本、貨量

資料來源：長榮，統一投顧整理 
2. 4Q26 進入傳統淡季，惟區域航線需求支撐營運 
第四季為貨櫃航運傳統淡季， 整體需求不如第三季旺季強勁， 惟
根據訂艙及裝載情況 ， 至11 月前貨量仍具支撐 。 北美航線方面 ，
美國主要港口進口貨櫃量維持高檔，零售庫存銷售比低於 1.3，
顯示零售商庫存水位相對偏低，仍有持續補貨需求，預期 11 月
貨量隨淡季趨緩 ，12 月則有農曆春節前備貨需求支撐 。 歐洲航線
方面 ，2026 年貨量成長優於預期 ， 雖運價自7 月後逐步回落 ， 但
紅海地緣政治風險尚未解除， 繞航仍持續消耗有效運力。 亞洲區
域航線第四季進入傳統旺季 ， 受惠中國加一帶動東南亞及南亞原
料、半成品運輸需求成長。此外，全球塞港造成約300 萬至 400
萬 TEU 有效運力損失，占全球總運力約 11%至 12%，搭配航商艙
位控管，有助支撐淡季運價。

預估 4Q26 營收 1,174.21 億元，-20.0%QoQ/+37.0%YoY，稅後淨
利 255.68 億元，-46.7%QoQ/+200.2%YoY，稅後 EPS 11.81 元。

3. 2027 年新船交付壓力增加，港口壅塞限制有效運力供給 
2027~2029 年為全球貨櫃船新船交付高峰，目前全球貨櫃船總運
力約3,400 萬TEU，至2030 年待交付新船運力超過1,400 萬TEU，
主流研調機構(Clarksons、Drewry)預估 2027 年名目運力供給增
速為 7.7~7.9%，供給成長壓力仍是 2027 年主要風險。惟名目運
力增加不代表有效運力等幅成長 ， 過去十年全球碼頭處理能力年
均成長約 2.7%，低於船隊運力年均成長約 5.4%，港口基礎設施
擴張速度落後船隊成長，塞港問題短期難以改善。紅海及蘇伊士
運河恢復通行亦為重要變數， 歐洲航線若恢復通行，單程約節省

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

7 天 、 往返約14 天 ， 但船舶集中抵港可能加劇碼頭壅塞 ， 抵銷部
分航程縮短釋出的運力。長榮截至 2030 年尚有 72 艘新船待交
付 ， 合計運力約89 萬 TEU， 並持續投資重要樞紐碼頭 ， 提升靠泊
效率及船隊調度彈性。

4. 2027 年歐美長約運價預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利 
2027 年北美及歐洲航線長約運價預期均高於 2026 年，主要受惠
三項因素 。 首先 ，2026 年第三季貨櫃運價大幅上漲 ， 目前現貨運
價明顯高於前一年度長約議價時的水準 ， 為新年度合約價格調升
奠定基礎 。 其次 ， 全球港口處理能力擴張速度落後船隊運力成長 ，
加上紅海繞航及主要港口壅塞持續消耗有效運力 ， 航商供給調節
能力提升，有利維持長約議價能力。最後，歐美航線貨運需求仍
具支撐 ， 北美零售商庫存水位偏低 ， 歐洲航線貨量成長優於預期 ，
提供長約價格上調條件。歐洲航線新約自 2027 年初開始適用，
北美航線則自 5 月起生效 ， 預期新年度長約運價調升將逐步反映
於營收，並有助減緩現貨運價波動對獲利的影響，支撐 2027 年
營運表現。

5. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 45.21/27.89 元，塞港及長約運
價調升支撐獲利高於歷史平均水準 
預估 2026/2027 年營收分別為 4,558.69／4,178.70 億元，分別
+20.3%YoY/-8.3%YoY，稅後淨利分別為 978.74／603.81 億元，
分別+42.7%YoY/-38.3%YoY，稅後 EPS 分別為 45.21 元／27.89
元。2026 年受惠第三季運價大幅上漲 ， 帶動全年獲利回升 。2027
年雖面臨新船交付增加及運價自高檔回落的壓力 ， 惟全球碼頭處
理能力成長落後船隊運力，加上塞港及紅海繞航限制有效供給，
歐美航線長約價格亦預期高於 2026 年 ， 有助減緩獲利下滑幅度 。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

附 錄 二 ： 免 責 宣 言

告 中 所 有 的 意 見 及 預 估 ， 如 有 變 更 恕 不 另 行 通 知 。

本 研 究 報 告 所 載 之 投 資 資 訊 ， 僅 提 供 客 戶 做 為 一 般 投 資 參 考 ， 並 非 針 對 特 定 對 象 提
供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之
著 作 權 ， 嚴 禁 抄 襲 與 仿 造 。

### Page 3

Largan Precision (3008.TW)
08 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Seeing multiple generation and years opportunities — Compared with these
broader solutions providers, Largan remains primarily as a high-precision
manufacturing and assembly specialist. Management described its current
business relationship as closer to contract manufacturing, with direct discussions
with end customers focused mainly on next-generation products. On product
architecture, the initial 2027 commercial project remains focused on single-row FA

using grating coupling (GC), while multi-row and multilayer configurations remain in
sampling and are primarily associated with 2028 programs. Management believes
its FA/FAU solution is broadly applicable to both CPO and NPO when the underlying
optical interface remains similar, because the primary difference is the placement of
the optical package. Largan’s primary differentiation is not the FA design itself but
its ability to manufacture highly precise assembly using imperfect input

components.
Automation and Yield Control Are the Real Competitive
Advantages
During the earnings call, Largan’s management emphasized that almost all critical
FA/FAU alignment equipment is developed internally, including machinery and
control software. This enables faster equipment modification, process
optimization and troubleshooting compared with labor-intensive assembly.
Automated production also offers better traceability and root-cause analysis when

yield problems emerge. Management stated that customer feedback is positive
and the FAU certification process progressing well. The key competitive advantage,
in our view, is the ability to combine submicron accuracy, high throughput and
reproducible yields at scale, rather than precision alone.
In our view, the earnings call supports a more constructive medium-term view of
Largan’s CPO opportunities than our previous assessment, particularly given the

apparent completion of initial FA qualification, customer interest in integrated FAU
assembly and a clearer path toward commercial production in 2H27. Moving from
standalone FA to FAU and potentially carrier assembly could materially increase
revenue content per optical connection, while the company’s automated
manufacturing capabilities may support differentiation in increasingly complex,
high-density optical architectures. The next growth opportunity could emerge from

multi-row FA and higher-channel-count optical solutions beginning in 2028,
particularly as AI networking bandwidth moves beyond current-generation
platforms.
Maintaining Buy on better FAU visibility
Given the weak smartphone order flow and GM pressure in the near term, we revise
down our net income projections by 9%/29% for 2026/2027, but slightly raise our
2028 net income projection by 1% given the solid growth opportunity in FAU
business. However, we think Largan is transitioning from a smartphone optics

manufacturer into a potential high-precision optical interconnect assembly
supplier. As they are moving to FAU not just FA; we assume higher revenue
contribution for 2027 /2028 of 28%/ 44% considering Largan could get 15%/30%
market share.

Largan Precision (3008.TW)
08 October 2026 Citi Research
8
Largan Precision
Company description
Largan is a plastic/glass/hybrid lens supplier in Taiwan with end-applications
including phone cameras, DSCs, MFPs, and tablets. Its new development into
FAU business should server as a long term growth opportunity.
Investment strategy
We rate Largan a Buy as we expect its core iPhone business to remain resilient
and its co-packaged optics (CPO) development to provide strong upside

potential in the longer term. We believe Largan could be a long-term winner in
the lens market driven by: 1) its technology leadership; 2) intellectual property
(IP) protection; and 3) higher yield vs. competitors. In the longer term, we
believe spec migration could keep Largan ahead of competitors.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$8,500 for Largan shares is based on 33x 2027 /28E
EPS. We are using 2027 /28E EPS average as we see new Fiber Array + PMLA

revenue to kick in from next year. Our 33x PER is higher than its historical 5-
year PER range, which we think is justified by its better order visibility proven
by its recent strong capacity expansion plan, and we expect its CPO business
engagement should provide a strong long-term growth opportunity.
Risks
Key downside risks include: 1) weaker smartphone demand or replacement
demand, and in particular, demand headwinds for certain key clients; 2)

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

E Ink Holdings Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales +5% MoM/
+14% YoY
In this report, we focus on E Ink's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$3,613mn (+5% MoM/+14% YoY).
• 3Q26 sales came in at NT$9,936mn (-3% QoQ/-5% YoY), in line with our
estimate at NT$9,985mn (-2% QoQ/-4% YoY) and the consensus at NT
$9,774mn (-4% QoQ/-6% YoY).
Our view:
• E Ink's monthly sales run rate will need to stay at the September level

through year end to achieve its full-year revenue guidance of up 10-15% YoY
for 2026. This implies its 4Q26 revenue will be up by 9% QoQ, which still
looks within reach, despite the softer seasonality
• While the worst seems to be behind us and valuation is at a trough level of
12x 2027e P/E, we see no strong catalysts in the near term, despite our long-
term positive view on  e-paper demand across various applications. Stay EW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
E Ink Holdings Inc. (8069.TWO)
Base case, 12x 2027e P/E. We believe this valuation methodology best captures E Ink's earn-
ings growth momentum. We view our target multiple of 12x as fair, considering our estimated
17% earnings CAGR for 2025-28. The implied PEG of 0.7x is largely in line with its typical level
in 2022-25, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 22x one-year forward consensus
EPS with a 37% earnings CAGR.

Risks to Upside
n Firmer-than-expected ESL orders
n Stronger demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Better macro conditions
n Milder competition
n Brisker inventory digestion
Risks to Downside
n Softer-than-expected ESL orders
n Slower demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Macro headwinds
n Intensified competition
n More protracted inventory digestion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

expect to see sequential MoM increase at 15%/20% MoM in Oct/Nov 2026 to 
NT$258m/ NT$309m, supported by capacity expansion, efficiency improvement, 
and rack-level AI server ramp up.

### Page 2

Earnings revision: Factoring in FOCI’s 3Q26 revenues, we revise down 2026E earnings 
by 6% on lower-than-expected CPO FAU revenues in 3Q, and revise up 2027E-30E 
earnings by 1%/1%/1%/1% mainly on higher revenues of high-speed CPO FAU, 
supported by FOCI’s focus on CPO-scale up and rising CPO switch demand.

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12m target price via a discounted P/E and derive 
the target P/E multiple via peers’ trading P/E vs. forward year EPS YoY (methodology 
unchanged). With this, our target multiple declines slightly to 16.4x (vs. 15.4x previously) 
based on FOCI’s largely unchanged long-term earnings growth (2030E-31E EPS YoY at 
15% on avg.), reflecting improving sentiment on rack-level AI server ramp up and 
high-speed transition. We continue to apply the target P/E to 2030E EPS (unchanged), 
and discount it back to 2027E via a 12.6% COE (unchanged). Our new TP is NT$900 (vs. 
NT$833 previously). Our new TP implies 51x 2027E P/E. Our new TP implies 10x 2027E 
P/S, within its historical trading range of 5x to 30x. Maintain Buy.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

3Q26 營收低於預期，營運逐季向上方向不變 
 2Q26 毛利率 28.4%， 低於預期 ，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元:雙鴻 2Q26 EPS 
10.41 元，低於元富預估的 13.72 元，獲利達成率 76%。2Q26 營收
87 億元，+2%QOQ，+64%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 28.42%，較 1Q26
衰退 1.2 個百分點，亦低於預估的 30.5%，主要是報廢品打消，預期
3Q26 毛利率回復至 1Q26 水準，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元。3Q26 營收
97.91 億元，+12.5%QOQ，+64.3%YOY，營收達成率 94%，低於
預期，主要是七月營收有遞延認列，實際3Q26 拉貨力道未如預期顯
著，預估 3Q26 毛利率 30%，預估 3Q26 EPS 14.13 元。 
 營運逐季成長 ， 氣冷及水冷兩塊業務均樂觀:AWS TR3 氣冷及水冷比
重至少是 70~80%對比 20~30%，TR4 將有機會為 100%全水冷，水
冷大趨勢不變 ，VR 9 月起量 ，TR3 9 月起量 Compute tray 及 Switch 
tray 及 Rack manifolds 都有相關產品出貨 ，ASIC 水冷產值高於 GPU
客戶，2H26~2027 年 ASIC 增長可期。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 1800 元：2Q26 毛利率低於預期為一次性因
素，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務樂觀，水冷擴張趨勢明確，故維持買進，預

雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
 雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
雙鴻成立於 1998 年 8 月 ， 為國內前二大NB 散熱模組廠 ， 過去主要生產散熱器 、
散熱片、散熱模組、散熱導管、風扇等，現在已轉型為提供客戶完整散熱解決
方案供應商。主要應用於筆記型電腦、桌上型電腦、智慧型手機及伺服器等領
域。2Q26 產品應用領域 Server 78%、PC 17%、VGA/Gaming 4%、及Others(手
機及車用) 1%。另外水冷業務比重於 1H26 約為 55~60%。

2Q26 毛利率 28.4%，低於預期，
2Q26 EPS 10.41 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 28.4%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元 
雙鴻 2Q26 EPS 10.41 元，低於元富預估的 13.72 元，獲利達成率 76%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 87 億元，+2%QOQ，+64%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 28.42%，較
1Q26 衰退 1.2 個百分點 ， 亦低於預估的30.5%， 主要是報廢品打消 ， 預期3Q26
毛利率回復上 1Q26 水準，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元。

雙鴻 3Q26 營收 97.91 億元，+12.5%QOQ，+64.3%YOY，營收達成率 94%，
低於預期，主要是七月營收有遞延認列，故推估基期過高，實際 3Q26 拉貨力
道未如預期顯著，預估 3Q26 毛利率 30%，預估 3Q26 EPS 14.13 元。

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊
業務均樂觀

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務均樂觀 
2025 年雙鴻水冷營收比重 35%，1Q26 提升至 55%，帶動公司業績大幅增長。
氣冷部分，2024 年月營收 2-2.5 億元，2025 年 4～4.5 億元，2026 年平均 6～
7 億元，氣冷水冷均樂觀，以 AWS TR3 來看，氣冷及水冷比重至少是 70~80%
對比 20~30%，TR4 將有機會為 100%全水冷 ， 水冷大趨勢不變 ，VR 9 月起量，
TR3 9 月起量 Compute tray 及 Switch tray 及 Rack manifolds 都有相關產品出
貨，Compute tray 水冷產值 3000usd 以上，Switch tray 水冷產值 1000usd 以
上，ASIC 水冷產值高於 GPU 客戶，2H26~2027 年 ASIC 增長可期。

1H26 GB 水冷營收中 Rack manifolds 佔比 30-35%、Cold Plate+inner manifolds
佔比 55-60%、CDU 5% ，2026 年可視為 CDU 元年，2027 年營收跳增可期。
公司展望 3Q26 營收+15~20%QOQ，實際+12.5%QOQ 低於預期，4Q26 營收
QOQ+雙位數，2026 年營收+70~85%YOY，3Q26 毛利率將回到 29.7%以上水
準，2027 年維持正向看法。

維持買進，目標價 1800 元

維持買進評等，目標價 1800 元 
2Q26 毛利率低於預期為一次性因素，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務樂觀，水冷擴張趨勢
明確，故維持買進，預估2026 年 EPS 55.74 元，預估 2027 年 EPS 90.36 元，
目標價 1800 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

## Document 189: 9月金融数据点评：政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月金融数据点评：政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳.pdf
Report source: 联储证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告 
宏观经济点评 2026 年 10 月 15 日

请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明

9 月金融数据点评：政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳

[Table_Author] 
 沈夏宜 分析师  魏争 分析师

Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com Email:weizheng@lczq.com  
证书:S1320523020004 证书:S1320524100001

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
外币贷款同比多增。9 月新增外币贷款 349 亿元，同比多增 478 亿元，可
能与当月进口增速偏强，企业进口付汇需求增加有关。委托贷款同比多
增。9 月新增委托贷款 802 亿元，同比多增 519 亿元。一方面，可能与新
型政策性金融工具开始投放有关。部分资金通过股东借款的形式投入项
目。相关基金委托政策性银行向项目公司股东提供借款，带动委托贷款增
加；另一方面，公积金贷款需求边际改善亦对委托贷款形成一定支撑。未
贴现银行承兑汇票同比少增。9 月未贴现银行承兑汇票增加 2153 亿元，
同比少增 1081 亿元，主要与季末银行加大收票力度，票据被贴现至表内
有关。 
M1 增速回落。9 月 M1 同比增长 3.2%，较上月回落 0.9 个百分点。或主
要受 9 月资本市场调整、市场风险偏好边际回落影响，资金活化程度有所
减弱。9 月非银存款亦减少 1.9 万亿元，同比多减 8500 亿元。 
M2 增速小幅回落。9 月 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较上月回落 0.3 个百分点。
一是信贷派生仍然偏弱，新增人民币贷款同比少增，银行体系通过信贷 派
生存款的能力受限；二是财政存款同比少减 4100 亿元，财政资金向企业
和居民存款转化力度偏弱，亦对 M2 形成一定拖累。 
政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳 。往前看，一是货币端，PSL 利率下调
至 1.5%，支持范围扩展至“六张网”，叠加科技创新和技术改造再贷
款、支农支小再贷款额度增加，有助于降低重点领域融资成本、拓宽重大
项目和薄弱环节的融资渠道，为后续信贷投放提供支撑 ；二是准财政端，
8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具已启动投放，项目资本金补充有望撬动配
套贷款及后续债券融资；三是财政端，5500 亿元地方政府债务结存限额
已安排使用，其中 2500 亿元专项债重点支持在建项目和“六张网”等领
域。叠加四季度政府债发行提速，相关政策效果有望逐步显现，对社融企
稳形成有效支撑。

风险提示：宏观经济不及预期，地产销售不及预期，美国货币政策超预
期，地缘政治风险超预期。

## Document 198: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다.

## Document 216: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

2026-09-30

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

## Document 226: AI信贷悖论：华尔街恐慌过早了吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI信贷悖论：华尔街恐慌过早了吗？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 相关债务规模预计到 2027 年底接近 2 万亿美元，但超⼤规模云计算商的平均信⽤评级不
降反升，信⽤利差却逼近年内⾼点。摩根⼤通指出，信贷压⼒主要集中于 AI 产业链中资产
负债表较弱的供应商，⽽⾮科技巨头。随着财报季临近，若巨头业绩稳健，本轮利差⾛阔
可能只是市场过度定价；但 AI 收⼊⾼度依赖内部资本⽀出循环，终端需求能否兑现仍是关
键考验。
摘要：
AI 投资热潮正在催⽣⼀个深层⽭盾：数万亿美元的基础设施承诺与尚未成熟的终端需求之间，裂
缝正在信贷市场悄然显现。
据摩根⼤通数据，超⼤规模云计算商（ hyperscaler ）信⽤利差上周扩⼤ 12 个基点⾄ 148 个基
点，创 7 ⽉以来最⾼⽔平，距年内⾼点仅差 6 个基点。与此同时， TS Lombard 估计，与 AI 相关的
累计债务规模到 2027 年底可能接近 2 万亿美元。信贷市场正在对 AI 融资⻛险重新定价。
然⽽，悖论恰在于此：超⼤规模云计算商今年债务规模虽增加了 1170 亿美元，其平均信⽤评级
却实际上⼩幅提升。信贷市场的压⼒更多集中于⽣态系统中的薄弱环节，⽽⾮那些主导融资的

科技巨头本⾝。随着财报季临近，市场⾯临的关键问题是：此轮利差扩⼤究竟预⽰着基本⾯的
实质恶化，还是不过是⼜⼀次仓位过度调整？
收⼊缺⼝： 3 万亿美元的悬念
AI 繁荣的核⼼⽭盾在于，谁来最终为这场盛宴买单。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

TS Lombard 估计，⽬前 AI ⽣态系统约 80% 的收⼊仍来源于内部资本⽀出的循环流转，⽽⾮来⾃
⽣态系统外部的真实终端需求。换⾔之，这场投资热潮在很⼤程度上仍是⾃我强化的闭环。
数字层⾯的压⼒同样不容忽视。 TS Lombard 援引估算指出，到 2030 年， AI 每年需要产⽣ 2.5 万
亿⾄ 3.5 万亿美元的收⼊，才能为当前持续加速的资本部署提供合理依据。⽽⽬前企业端收⼊估
计仅约 2000 亿美元。这⼀巨⼤缺⼝能否弥合，以及弥合的速度，将是决定整个 AI 融资逻辑能否
⾃洽的核⼼变量。
信贷分化：恐慌还是精准定价？
信贷市场的反应并⾮⽆差别的恐慌，⽽是呈现出⽇益明显的选择性。
摩根⼤通数据显⽰，投资级信⽤利差离散度已升⾄ 2025 年 5 ⽉以来的最⾼⽔平。投资者正在将资
产负债表稳健的公司与那些深度暴露于 AI 融资热潮的企业加以区分。压⼒最为集中的，是为 AI ⽣
态系统提供基础设施、算⼒和电⼒的中⼩型供应商，⽽⾮超⼤规模云计算商本⾝。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
相⽐之下，更⼴泛的投资级市场表现出更强的韧性。尽管近期通胀预期有所升温，投资级债券
收益率上周实际下⾏了 22 个基点。这⼀背离表明，市场对 AI 融资⻛险的担忧⽬前仍属局部性、
结构性，尚未演变为系统性的信⽤收缩。
财报季：下⼀个关键节点
即将到来的财报季将是检验信贷市场判断的重要试⾦⽯。
摩根⼤通指出，今年夏季超⼤规模云计算商的财报发布恰好对应了信⽤利差的阶段性⾼点，此
后利差随之收窄。该⾏认为，本轮利差扩⼤存在类似反转的可能性。若超⼤规模云计算商财报
表现稳健，且收益率维持在可控区间，近期的信⽤利差⾛阔或将再度被证明是⼀次仓位过度调
整，⽽⾮基本⾯恶化的前兆。
核⼼悖论依然存在： AI 融资热潮的裂缝正在显现，但信贷市场或许已经⾛在了基本⾯前⾯。最⼤
的脆弱性并不必然来⾃主导这场投资浪潮的科技巨头，⽽是潜伏于⽣态系统中那些资产负债表

更为薄弱的供应链环节。在利差逼近夏季⾼点、财报季即将到来之际，市场的答案或许很快揭
晓。

## Document 232: AI资金流向图：英伟达拿走6900亿，其余499家瓜分3400亿

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI资金流向图：英伟达拿走6900亿，其余499家瓜分3400亿.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

摩根⼠丹利⾸次绘制 AI 资本⽀出全景图： 2027 年超⼤规模云服务商 1.58 万亿美元⽀出中，
英伟达独吞 67% ，其余 499 家 S&P 成分股合计仅分得 3390 亿美元。更隐秘的⻛险在于：逾
1.1 万亿美元的 " 表外租约 " 实为变相债务， 2027 年将是⾃由现⾦流缺⼝最⼤之年，量价同时
下⾏的⻛险正被市场严重低估。
摘要：
摩根⼠丹利⾸次绘制出 2027 年 AI 资本⽀出的完整流向图，揭⽰出⼀个⾼度集中的收益结构：在
超⼤规模云服务商 1.58 万亿美元的⽀出中，约三分之⼆的 S&P 500 受益⾦额流向英伟达⼀家，其
余 499 家成分股合计仅分得约 3390 亿美元。
摩根⼠丹利策略师 Nick Lentini 、 Mike Wilson 及逾⼆⼗位⾏业分析师联合发布报告《追踪 AI 1.6
万亿美元资⾦流向：绘制资本⽀出繁荣所创造的收⼊图谱》，⾸次将超⼤规模云服务商的资本⽀
出逐笔追踪⾄具体公司的收⼊报表。报告核⼼结论是：AI 资本⽀出将直接驱动 S&P 500 约 25%

的销售增⻓， AI 相关企业将贡献 2027 年标普指数 78% 的盈利增⻓。
报告同时揭⽰了⼀个⻓期被忽视的隐性⻛险：超⼤规模云服务商已披露逾 1.1 万亿美元尚未启动
的租约承诺，这部分 " 合成资本⽀出 " 并不体现在任何现⾦流量表的资本⽀出项下，却在经济实质
上等同于债务融资。摩根⼠丹利信⽤策略师预计，2027 年将是超⼤规模云服务商⾃由现⾦流缺
⼝最⼤的⼀年，届时债务融资压⼒将显著上升。
1.6 万亿美元的全景地图
摩根⼠丹利报告的核⼼是⼀张桑基图，将九⼤买家 ——Alphabet 、亚⻢逊 AWS 、微软、 Meta 、
租约池、 SpaceX 、甲⻣⽂、 CoreWeave 及 Nebius—— 合计 1.58 万亿美元的 2027 年⽀出，逐层
追踪⾄半导体与服务器、⽹络与光学、数据中⼼基础设施、存储及国际供应商，最终落地⾄具
体股票代码。
九⼤买家中，半导体与服务器是最⼤的单⼀⽀出⽅向，吸收 6720 亿美元，其中绝⼤部分流向英
伟达。第⼆⼤⽅向是 " 国际 " 供应商，规模达 5110 亿美元，涵盖三星、 SK 海⼒⼠、台积电相关基

础设施及台湾 ODM ⼚商，这些公司均不在 S&P 500 成分股之列。
报告还指出，整张地图将 1.6 万亿美元资本⽀出映射⾄约 1.7 万亿美元的供应商收⼊，差额来源于
私有新兴云服务商和⽐特币矿商对客⼾拆分的⼲扰、时间差异，以及尚未反映进资本⽀出预测
的供应商涨价。摩根⼠丹利的解读是：下⼀轮资本⽀出上调已经内嵌于供应商模型之中。
英伟达独吞三分之⼆，其余 499 家均分残局
在 S&P 500 从 AI 资本⽀出中吸收的约 1.03 万亿美元收⼊⾥， 67% 流向英伟达，折合约 6900 亿美
元，相当于整个 1.58 万亿美元资本⽀出预算的 44% 落在⼀家公司的利润表上。剔除英伟达后，
整个 AI 资本⽀出繁荣对 S&P 500 销售额的贡献仅为 1.6% 。
粗略拆算：约 7000 亿美元归属英伟达，约 3400 亿美元由其余 499 家 S&P 成分股共享，另有约
5500 亿美元流出指数之外，流向韩国存储⼚商、台湾 ODM 及各类私有企业。
在个股层⾯，摩根⼠丹利调查了各公司 2027 年收⼊中与 AI 基础设施建设挂钩的⽐例。英伟达以

91.7% 居⾸，其后依次为 Erock （ EROC ） 91% 、 Vertiv （ VRT ） 90% 、 Applied Opto （ AAOI ）
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

83% 、博通（ AVGO ） 81% 、 Bloom Energy （ BE ） 80% 。即便是卡特彼勒（ CAT ），也有 21%
的收⼊与 AI 资本⽀出趋势相关；希捷（ STX ）和西部数据（ WDC ）均达 60% 。
这⼀结构意味着，对于⾄少⼗余家⼤型公司⽽⾔， "AI 资本⽀出 " 与 " 收⼊ " ⼏乎已是同义词 —— 上
⾏时锦上添花，下⾏时资本⽀出的任何修正都将直接转化为盈利修正，没有其他业务可以缓
冲。
1950 亿美元的隐性负债
报告最重要的⽅法论修正之⼀，是将租约纳⼊资本⽀出统计。摩根⼠丹利利⽤公司披露的已签
署但尚未启动的租约、分析师对新增产能的预测，以及每 GW 新增产能对应的租约资本⽀出⽐
率，为 2027 年资本⽀出新增 1950 亿美元， 2028 年新增 2460 亿美元。
这将 2027 年 AI 资本⽀出总额从 1.39 万亿美元（现⾦资本⽀出）提升⾄ 1.58 万亿美元，同⽐增速
约 60% ； 2028 年则从 1.57 万亿美元升⾄ 1.81 万亿美元。

在租约规模上，甲⻣⽂以 570 亿美元居⾸，其次为微软（ 450 亿美元）、 Meta （ 370 亿美元）、亚
⻢逊（ 280 亿美元）和 Alphabet （ 270 亿美元）。截⾄ 2026 年第⼆季度，已签署但尚未启动的租
约管道已膨胀⾄ 1.118 万亿美元，仅两个季度便翻逾⼀倍，微软（ 3290 亿美元）、甲⻣⽂（ 2880
亿美元）和 Meta （ 2790 亿美元）是主要签约⽅。
摩根⼠丹利的定性判断是：租赁数据中⼼在经济实质上与债务融资的资本⽀出完全相同，只是
绕过了 GAAP 资本⽀出科⽬。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
43 收藏 分享到 :
债务：被低估的第⼀环
摩根⼠丹利信⽤策略师 Fernanda Lima 和 Vishwas Patkar 预计，超⼤规模云服务商和新兴云服务
商的⾃由现⾦流将在 2027 年触及最负值，若计⼊租约⽀付，资⾦缺⼝将更⼤。
超⼤规模云服务商今年迄今已发⾏约 2550 亿美元⽆担保债券，约占其 2026 年预估资本⽀出的
30% ，亚⻢逊（ 980 亿美元）和 Alphabet （ 810 亿美元）领跑，⽽综合收益率⾃年初以来已上升
100 ⾄ 180 个基点，甲⻣⽂ 10 年期债券利差扩张最为显著，达 180 个基点。
摩根⼠丹利的压⼒测试显⽰，若 AA 评级的超⼤规模云服务商以 7% 利率发⾏ 4000 亿美元债券，
利息覆盖倍数将从 30.7 倍降⾄仍属舒适的 11.9 倍；但若甲⻣⽂和 SpaceX 以 8.5% 利率融资 1200 亿

美元，两者合并利息覆盖倍数将从 4.0 倍骤降⾄ 1.8 倍。
报告明确指出，新兴云服务商对债务融资的依赖程度最⾼， " ⼏乎完全依赖债务发⾏来⽀撑其资
本⽀出雄⼼ " 。报告发布数⽇前， CoreWeave 股价已⼤幅下挫，⽽该公司在摩根⼠丹利的资本⽀
出地图中对应 470 亿美元的⽀出规模。
增速放缓与 " 电⼒墙 "
摩根⼠丹利预计，AI 资本⽀出增速将从 2027 年的约 60% ⼤幅放缓⾄ 2028 年的约 14% 。在此背景
下，报告筛选出在资本⽀出增速趋缓时仍具备定价权的标的，电⼒设备类占据主导，存储、硬
盘及冷却设备同样⼊列。
电⼒供应是另⼀个结构性约束。摩根⼠丹利⾃有的 "AI 供电 " 模型显⽰，即便考虑各类快速供电解
决⽅案（覆盖所需 97GW 中的 34% ），美国本⼟ 2028 年前仍⾯临 32GW 的净缺⼝， 2029 年前缺⼝
扩⼤⾄约 72GW 。报告将此定性为时序错配⽽⾮需求崩塌，但指出存储、⽹络、光学和 ODM 建
设对供电暂停最为敏感。
在下⾏⻛险⽅⾯，摩根⼠丹利措辞罕⻅直⽩：当前市场存在 " 担⼼错过采购 " 的⼼理，买家今⽇⾼

价抢购是因为预期明⽇更贵，⼀旦供给追上需求，这⼀逻辑将逆转。报告援引 2022 年 NAND 内
存周期为例 —— 出货量仅下滑约 5% ，但价格跌幅达 20% ⾄ 30%—— 并得出结论： " 我们认为，当
前市场低估了量价同时下⾏的⻛险。 "
摩根⼠丹利的报告⾸次为市场提供了⼀张逐美元追踪的 AI 资本⽀出流向图，清晰呈现出 S&P 500
约 25% 的销售增⻓和 78% 的盈利增⻓，依赖于⼀笔 1.6 万亿美元的资本⽀出预算 —— ⽽这笔预算
中，指数层⾯三分之⼆的受益集中于⼀家公司，且越来越⼤的⽐例通过不计⼊资本⽀出的租约
和需要再融资才会显现的债务来⽀撑。

2 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

### Page 6

6 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.
Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.

of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

内容提要 
2026 年 9 月份，CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较前月上涨 0.1 个百分点，这主要受能源价格上
涨推动，居民消费价格整体运行平稳，温和上涨。9 月能源价格上涨明显，是推动CPI 增速
上行的主要因素。 
2026 年 9 月份，PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较前月上涨 1.2 个百分点，上游原材料价格上涨，
尤其能源价格再次上涨， 推动工业品价格增速继续上行。 一方面，8 月以来的美伊战争加剧，
国际能源价格再次上涨； 另一方面， 促消费叠加整治反“反内卷” 式竞争， 耐用消费品价格
增速明显上涨。 
9 月国际能源价格继续上涨，中国国内通胀率上行。具体各项数据间接反映出，本月经
济结构调整继续，高技术产业继续快速增长，终端需求相对旺盛，价格增速继续上行。9 月
CPI 中通信工具价格同比增长 14.3%， 较前月上涨3.7 个百分点；PPI 中计算机、 通信和其他
电子设备制造业价格同比增长 5.8%，较前月上涨 0.5 个百分点。

上行的主要因素。

北京大学国民经济研究中心 
能源价格波动，PPI 增速上行 
2026 年 9 月份，PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较前月上涨 1.2 个百分点，上游原材料价格上涨，
尤其能源价格再次上涨，推动工业品价格增速继续上行。一方面，8 月以来的美伊战争加剧，
国际能源价格再次上涨； 另一方面， 促消费叠加整治反“反内卷” 式竞争， 耐用消费品价格
增速明显上涨。 
8 月以来的美伊战争加剧，使得国际原油价格上涨，9 月国际原油平均价格明显高于 8
月，能源价格继续大幅上涨。9 月 PPI 中，石油和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工
业价格分别同比增长 24.5%、21.6%，较 8 月上涨 10.0、10.5 个百分点，本月能源及相关工
业品价格增速继续明显上涨，是推动本月 PPI 增速上涨的主要因素。

是原油价格上涨，是导致本月 PPI 增速上行的主要因素。生产资料价格同比增长 6.5%，较
前月上涨 1.5 个百分点，其中采掘业、原材料、加工业价格同比增速分别上涨 7.6、2.7、0.3
个百分点； 生活资料价格同比下降0.1%，降幅较前月收窄 0.4 个百分点，其 中耐用消费品价
格增速上涨 0.4 个百分点，食品、一般日用品价格增速也较前月上行。

### Page 8

北京大学国民经济研究中心
CPI 方面，深入整治 “反内卷” 式竞争或能缓解供给相对过剩的问题， 同时受 “稳增长、
促消费” 政策影响，叠加 2025 年的低基数效应， 或推动2026 年 CPI 增速上行。经济内生动
力不足， 国内经济压力依然较大， 就业、 收入预期并未发生明显改变， 居民有效需求不足问
题掣肘，抑制居民消费价格短期快速大幅上涨，但若中东战争的影响持续扩大， 或加速通胀
率的上行。综合预计 2026 年 CPI 增速或温和上涨。
PPI 方面，一方面，国内外环境进一步复杂多变，能源、矿产争夺加剧，或推动上游原
材料价格上涨，政策调整， “依法依规治理企业低价无序竞争”， 叠加低基数效应， 拉动2026
年 PPI 增速上行； 另一方面，中国国内经济压力犹存， 企业内生动力不足， 工业有效需求有
待刺激， 房地产市场产能去化犹在， 经济结构调整继续， 抑制需求端扩张。 但若中东战争的
影响持续扩大，或产生输入性通胀，工业品价格上涨或加速。综合预计 2026 年 PPI 增速或

震荡上行。

### Page 9

北京大学国民经济研究中心 
北京大学国民经济研究中心简介： 
北京大学国民经济研究中心成立于 2004 年，挂靠在北京大学经济学院。依托北京大学，重点研
究领域包括中国经济波动和经济增长、宏观调控理论与实践、经济学理论、中国经济改革实践、转轨
经济理论和实践前沿课题、政治经济学、西方经济学教学研究等。同时，本中心密切跟踪宏观经济与
政策的重大变化， 将短期波动和长期增长纳入一个综合性的理论研究框架， 以独特的观察视角去解读，
把握宏观趋势、剖析数据变化、理解政策初衷、预判政策效果。 
中心的研究取得了显著的成果， 对中国的宏观经济政策产生了较大影响。 其中最具有代表性的成
果有： （1）推动了中国人口政策的调整。中心主任苏剑教授从 2006 年开始就呼吁中国应该立即彻底
放弃计划生育政策， 并转而鼓励生育。 （2） 关于宏观调控体系的研究： 中心提出了包括市场化改革、
供给管理和需求管理政策的三维宏观调控体系。 （3） 关于宏观调控力度的研究：2017 年 7 月， 本中
心指出中国的宏观调控应该严防用力过猛， 这一建议得到了国务院主要领导的批示， 也与三个月后十
九大报告中提出的“宏观调控有度”的观点完全一致。（4）关于中国经济目标增速的成果。2013 年，
刘伟、 苏剑经过缜密分析和测算， 认为中国每年只要有6.5%的经济增速就可以确保就业。 此后不久，
这一增速就成为中国政府经济增长速度的基准目标。 最近几年中国经济的实践也证明了他们的这一测
算结果的精确性。 （5）供给侧研究。刘伟和苏剑教授是国内最早研究供给侧的学者，他们在 2007 年
就开始在 《经济研究》 等杂志上发表关于供给管理的学术论文。 （6） 新常态研究。 刘伟和苏剑合作的
论文“新常态下的中国宏观调控”（ 《经济科学》2014 年第 4 期） 是研究中国经济新常态的第一篇学术
论文。 苏剑和林卫斌还研究了发达国家的新常态。 （7） 刘伟和苏剑主编的 《寻求突破的中国经济》 被
译成英文、韩文、俄文、日文、印地文 5 种文字出版。（8）北京地铁补贴机制研究。2008 年，本课
题组受北京市财政局委托设计了北京市地铁运营的补贴机制。该机制从 2009 年 1 月 1 日开始被使
用，直到现在。 
中心出版物有： （1） 《原富》杂志。 《原富》是一个月度电子刊物，由北京大学国民经济研究中
心主办，目的是以最及时、最专业、 最全面的方式呈现本月国内外主要宏观经济大事并对重点事件进
行专业解读。 （2） 《中国经济增长报告》 （年度报告） 。该报告主要分析中国经济运行中存在的中长
期问题，从 2003 年开始已经连续出版 14 期，是相关年度报告中连续出版年限最长的一本， 被教育部
列入其年度报告资助计划。（3）系列宏观经济运行分析和预测报告。本中心定期发布关于中国宏观
经济运行的系列分析和预测报告，尤其是本中心的预测报告在预测精度上在全国处于领先地位。

## Document 312: GF - PTFE Adoption

Source PDF: all report pdf/GF - PTFE Adoption.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Equity Research 
Sector Report | Overseas Technology｜Oct 15,2026 
PTFE Adoption: 
Reshape the High-End Materials Landscape

Overseas  
Technology

Michelle Jing 
SFC CE No. BUK594 
michellejing@gfgroup.com.hk 
Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk

What's new 
Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE board prototype for the first time at OCP Global Summit 2026, highlighting seven supply-chain partners: 
Taiflex (8039 TT), Flexium (6269 TT), TTM Technologies (TTMI US), 3 leading Chinese PCB manufacturers, and 1 leading Chinese CCL 
supplier. We believe this development reinforces our previous view that PTFE remains the most promising material solution for the VR300 
NVL576 switch board, offering the best balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
Comments 
PTFE hybrid remains the preferred solution: As highlighted in our Oct. 5 report, we continue to favor the PTFE hybrid solution for the 
VR300 NVL576 switch board among the various options, given its optimal balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
While pure PTFE remains challenging to process, particularly in lamination and drilling, leading PCB manufacturers have achie ved 
encouraging yields with hybrid structures that combine PTFE signal layers with M9 materials in non-signal layers. 
We like PTFE's long-term adoption potential: PTFE applications in high-frequency and high-speed interconnects could extend beyond 
switch boards. The IEC has published a standard on PTFE-filled CCL, led by the leading Chinese CCL company, highlighting the material's 
relevance to broader circuit-board applications. Our checks also suggest that PTFE materials have entered the testing phase for other 
applications, including compute boards. We therefore remain positive on PTFE's long-term adoption across a broader range of high-end 
PCB applications. 
Limited near-term impact on conventional resin materials, but long-term substitution risks warrant attention: Under the current 
PTFE hybrid design, we expect 8–12 out of 32 layers to adopt PTFE for signal transmission, accounting for less than 50% of the total CCL 
resin content value. Over the longer term, we expect a potential shift toward pure PTFE solutions as improvements in processing 
technology enable commercially viable MP yields, reducing the need for hybrid structures. This could gradually increase substitution risks 
for conventional high-end PCB resin materials, including PPO and HC resin. 
Favor PTFE-exposed suppliers with proven technical and manufacturing capabilities: We remain positive on PTFE-related 
opportunities, particularly suppliers with strong R&D capabilities, proven MP expertise, and established customer qualifications. In CCL, 
we favor Taiflex (8039 TT) and leading Chinese CCL supplier for their PTFE material capabilities and supply-chain exposure. Among the 
five Nvidia-announced PCB suppliers, we favor the leading Chinese PTFE PCB supplier, given its advanced hybrid PTFE processing 
capabilities and encouraging yields. For CCL suppliers that have yet to enter the PTFE supply chain, we recommend monitoring progress 
in developing, while remaining alert to potential market-share losses in their conventional product portfolios. 
Risks  
1) AI demand slowing down; 2) Geo-political risks; 3) Competition.

### Page 2

Sector Report | Overseas Technology

Figure 1: NVLink PTFE Board

## Document 344: NZ forecast update, October 2026 (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ forecast update, October 2026 (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• Higher oil and refined fuels prices, a lower
exchange rate, higher long term interest rates
and weak house price trends justify a review of
our 2026 forecasts.
• We continue to see CPI inflation falling to 3.7%
y/y in the September quarter, but now think
inflation will rise to 4.2% in the December
quarter. Higher global fuel prices and a weaker
New Zealand dollar are driving these trends.
• Commodity export prices remain robust and

are reflected in a rise in our forecast for the
2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price to $9.75/kg. Our
opening bid for the Milk Price in 2027/28 is a
solid $9.50/kg.
• House prices have not stabilised as expected
in recent months. As a result, we have revised
down our 2026 house price forecast from a
small rise to a 1.4% decline.
• The combination of high fuel costs and a weak
housing market will likely crimp consumer
demand running into Christmas.
• We have modestly revised down our GDP

forecasts for the December quarter, which
reduces our 2026 growth forecast to a still
solid 2.5%. Risks appear skewed to the
downside given higher energy prices and long-
term interest rates.
• We will look at our 2027 forecasts in the
coming week.
• Our OCR forecasts remain unchanged. We see
a pause at the October meeting and a hike to
3% at the release of the December Monetary
Policy Statement.
Inflation – All gas, no brakes.
We’ve updated our near-term inflation forecasts in

response to recent global developments.
We continue to estimate that consumer prices rose by
0.7% in the September quarter, consistent with recent
monthly prices updates from Stats NZ. That would see
annual inflation ease to 3.7% in the year to September,
down from 4.1% in the year to June. We’ll confirm that
figure next week, when the final monthly prices update
for the September quarter is released on 16 October.

However, that easing in inflation appears set to be only
a temporary reprieve. We now expect consumer prices
to rise 1.1% in the December quarter. That would see
annual inflation rising to a three-year high of 4.2% by

the end of this year. The main driver of this revision are
sharply higher global fuel prices, compounded by a more
than 4% fall in the New Zealand dollar since the start of
September. Together, those developments have already
pushed local petrol prices up around 50 cents / litre over
the past month, with the nationwide average price of 91
unleaded reaching a record $3.53 / litre earlier this week.
9 Oct 2026 | Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

RBNZ target range
Our updated year-end inflation forecast is above the
RBNZ’s September projection of 3.9%. In a recent
presentation, Governor Breman noted that the rise in oil
prices meant the risks to the RBNZ’s near-term inflation
forecast were to the upside. However, she also noted that

the RBNZ’s focus was on the medium-term outlook.
We haven’t changed our quarterly inflation forecasts for
2027 at this stage. However, the upward revision to our
forecast for the December quarter means annual inflation
is set to remain firm through most of 2027, consistent
with the RBNZ’s forecasts. We will review our 2027
forecasts for inflation and growth more comprehensively
next week when we will also review our financial forecasts
with the release of the Westpac Market Outlook.

A more tepid housing market.
We had expected the housing market to stabilise through
the second half of 2026. However, recent trends have
disappointed. House sales volumes continue to weaken,
and the median number of days required to sell a house
has lengthened to 48 days. Not surprisingly, therefore,

average house prices have continued to nudge lower
despite more robust conditions in those areas – mostly
in the South Island – that are most exposed to the well-
performing export sector. The latest indicators suggest
no imminent change in this trend.
Given these weak trends, our previous forecast for
essentially flat house prices through the September and
December quarters now looks too optimistic. We have
revised down our house price forecast for the 2026 year

from a rise of 0.6% y/y to a fall of 1.4% y/y on a national
basis. This is consistent with quarterly falls in house
prices of 0.5% and 0.8% q/q over the September and
December quarters.
The still weak labour market, higher mortgage rates and
still moderate employment growth all point to a weak
housing market for the foreseeable future. Risks remain
tilted towards another weak year in 2027. This would
especially be the case if increased taxation of capital

gains on investor housing were to occur as a result of the
outcome of the 2026 General Election.

It’s likely to be the case that there will be divergent trends
in the housing market across the country. Income and
employment trends in tourism and agriculture dominated

regions are expected to remain robust. Our upgrade to
the 2026/27 Milk Price released in our recent Agri Bites
report emphasises this point. But it looks like it will be
a while before investors and homeowners in the major
metropolitan areas in the North Island will be feeling
wealthier through a rising home value. On balance this
is likely to crimp consumer spending attitudes through
the end of 2026 and might moderate the recovery in the
construction sector that is underway.

Growth forecasts moderated a touch.
Our short-term growth forecasts still appear to be in
the ballpark of what seems likely given recent trends.
Recent consumer and business confidence surveys have

suggested resilience of the economy to recent rises in
energy prices and long-term interest rates. However, it’s
likely the rise in energy prices in particular will eat into
consumer spending appetites towards the end of 2026
should current levels of fuel prices be sustained.
We have knocked off a modest amount off our forecast
for consumer spending in the December quarter to
reflect these risks and will look more closely at the
implications for 2027 growth in coming weeks. Certainly,

the large rise in long term interest rates may be relevant
for 2027 growth trends. The election outcome introduces
two-sided risks to the 2027 growth outlook that are hard
to quantify at this early stage. We currently expect 2.5%
y/y growth for the 2026 year.
We don’t see a need to change our labour market
forecasts for 2026 at this point. Signs continue to
accumulate suggesting strengthening employment
growth – although it will be a while before a noticeable

drop in the unemployment rate is evident.
Implications for the OCR.
We are leaving our forecasts of the OCR unchanged.
Since the July 2026 Monetary Policy Review, we have
anticipated the RBNZ will slow the pace of OCR increases
now the 3% neutral level (in the RBNZ’s view) is coming
into view.
The RBNZ sent a clear signal that they expected to pause
in October and raise the OCR just one more time in
December. We still think this is the most likely scenario.

The recent data has boosted the short-term inflation
profile but at this stage there is little evidence of rising
inflation expectations or more persistent inflationary
pressures. There are some risks to the growth outlook –
albeit two sided – to consider. Taking the time to assess
the impact of the OCR rises to date and the strategy going
forward makes sense to us. We don’t see a shift in the
MPC’s strategy in October.
We continue to think it most likely that the RBNZ will

have more work to do in 2027. However, there is a lot
of water to go under the bridge before then. The OCR
increases that have occurred to date, and the one further
expected increase in December will position the MPC
well to respond to conditions in 2027 as they unfold.
The unexpected rise in long term interest rates that
has occurred in the last 6 weeks or so has tightened
monetary conditions and has at least offset the easing in
conditions coming from the weaker exchange rate over

the same period.

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Markets have had little respite over the past month. Hopes 
of a lasting US–Iran agreement have faded and intermittent 
attacks on shipping have kept Brent oil prices in triple digits. 
Workarounds have allowed a meaningful share of crude to 
continue flowing but these arrangements are slower and 
costlier, while depleted inventories leave little room to 
absorb another shock and a range of disruptions to refining 
and shipping are also impacting downstream energy costs. 
Bond markets have been just as restless, with continued lifts 
in government bond yields across much of the globe and 
more sharply in the US. 
Notably, oil is no longer doing the heavy lifting for yields. 
The rise has been concentrated in real yields, with the Fed’s 
September hike – and its signal of more to come – acting as 
something of a trigger. In the US, fiscal concerns have been 
more of a sub-plot, though the Treasury’s efforts to hold 
down long yields have arguably only drawn more attention 
to them. 
In our view, markets are getting ahead of themselves on 
monetary policy offshore. We expect just one more hike 
from the Fed rather than the three the market currently has 
priced, with stretched housing affordability posing risks to 
both consumption and inflation. Central banks in Europe, the 
UK and Canada are also likely to bide their time after a final 
move. Europe is weathering the energy shock better than 
feared and has far more fiscal room to respond than the US – 
one reason we still see the US dollar trending lower over the 
next two years.
The RBA hiked again in September and looks set to follow 
up in November, taking the cash rate to a new cycle high. 
Some of the risks the Board flagged have crystallised, 
but the minutes also reveal a tendency to reach for the 
more hawkish read of the data, in addition to the familiar 
downbeat take on supply capacity. Our base case assumes 
no return to full-scale conflict, but with energy buffers 
depleted, bond markets less forgiving and the RBA already 
leaning hawkish, the margin for error – for markets and 
policymakers alike – is starting to ‘wear thin’.
Australia: After a soft but better than expected first half, the 
Australian economy is facing into more challenges as another 
round of disruptions from global energy markets combine with 
further interest rate rises and a deepening housing downturn. 
The demand mix should see capacity pressures continue to 
ease but with significant risks on either side.
Middle East: Low inventories and higher shipping costs will 
keep oil and fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast 
to average US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by 
Q4 2027 as supply recovers. Risks are two-sided: escalation 
could push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran 
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply.
Commodities: Most commodities rose in September, with 
many of the themes that drove moves across August remaining 
in place. Japanese LNG and Brent oil recorded strong gains 
amid Middle East tensions. Copper and aluminium extended 
recent gains while iron ore was supported by higher freight 
and production costs. Gold fell after the Fed’s September rate 
hike. 
Global FX Markets: In late September and early-October 
the DXY index popped higher to near 103, a high back to 
early-2025. Underlying the move are expectations that 
inflation will prove persistent globally as well as a belief that 
the US’ tech sector leaves it best positioned to weather the 
storm.
New Zealand: Higher fuel costs and a weaker exchange rate 
have lifted the short-term inflation outlook while the economy 
has continued to grow through the Iran war. Growth looks set 
to continue to be buoyed by strong commodity prices and a 
supportive exchange rate. However, downside risks are growing 
from higher energy costs and long-term interest rates. 
United States: The US housing market remains under 
considerable pressure. Prices continue to edge up, supporting 
wealth. But up-and-coming main street consumers are 
struggling with affordability and availability, posing enduring 
risks for both consumption growth and inflation.   
China: With each month that passes, the risk of disappointing 
authorities’ 4.5-5.0% ambition grows. While the Government 
is right to take a targeted approach to support, it must quickly 
multiply its scale if downside risks are to be kept at bay. 
Asia: For much of the past twenty years, Asia has been a 
source of disinflation for the world. But a combination of ageing 
populations, rising labour costs, increasing electricity demand 
and technology sector developments imply Asia may provide 
less support to global disinflation over the decade ahead.
Europe & UK: The euro has come under considerable pressure 
during the past month. That said, the European economy 
continues to tick along nicely while inflation risks remain 
manageable, allowing the ECB to be measured in their response.

Wearing thin

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
The main development affecting global markets this month
has been the sharp lift in selected bond yields, especially in
the US and France. Since the previous Market Outlook was
published, 10-year benchmarks have risen 27bps for US

Treasuries, to be well above the 5% threshold, and 40bps for
French bonds. The latter move contrasts with German bunds,
which have seen little change in yields at that tenor over the
month, though they are up more than 60bps year to date.
Several factors are behind the latest rise, with no single
cause dominating. For some of these factors, the underlying
conditions had been in evidence for some time, but recent
events focused attention on them. The rise has been primarily

a story of real yields: longer-run inflation expectations implied
by both breakevens and swaps have not increased that much.
For much of the year, fluctuations in oil prices – and thus the
near-term inflation outlook – fed into market views of central
bank policy, and longer yields as well. More recently, though,
this correlation has broken down. Shifts in expectations about
central bank policy have remained important for bond pricing,
as evident in the concentration of the increase in real yields.

The FOMC’s decision to increase the Fed funds rate, and flag in
the ‘dot plots’ that at least one more hike was likely, seems to
have been something of a trigger event for repricing, not only
for US yields. Also salient were the various actions taken by
the US Treasury to suppress long yields and improve the US
government’s debt-servicing position. These efforts had little
lasting effect and yields are now higher, and higher relative to
peers, than before. But they may have served to draw attention

to the growing issues around US fiscal sustainability. High
supply of debt from hyperscaler firms to fund the AI / data
centre boom has also likely pressured yields at the margin.
That said, fiscal concerns are unlikely to have been the main
issue driving the lift in US yields. The slope of the US yield
curve is noticeably flatter than at the beginning of the year;
in other words, short yields have risen even more than the
10-year yield, signifying tighter expected monetary policy

lifting yields across the curve. Another lens on this shift is that
2-year and other short-dated yields have increased more than
estimates of risk-neutral rate, such as that from the NY Fed.
Fiscal concerns have been more salient in the case of France,
where spreads to bunds have widened from around 70bps at
the beginning of the year to nearly 140bps currently, wider than
Greece or Italy, with about 40bps of the widening occurring in
the past month. Contagion to other euro area sovereigns has

been limited, with spreads widening around 22bps over the
month for Greece, Italy and Belgium, and 13bps for Spain.
Many factors lifting global bond yields ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... and RBA policy rates outlook
In Australia, the RBA hiked the cash rate again at its late
September meeting, and a further hike at the November
meeting looks likely. This would take the cash rate to 4.85%, a
new cycle high.
While the forecasts the RBA published in August did not

support further rate hikes, in September these forecasts were
characterised as resting on several things going right and
certain risks not emerging. Some of these risks, including a re-
escalation of the war in the Middle East and thus energy prices,
have since crystallised. The post-meeting communication
and minutes also pointed to the July CPI result being above
expectations, as was June quarter GDP growth “at the margin”.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction

costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data
centre boom. No weight is put on the possibility that this new
investment and technology might add to productivity or the
capital stock, and so supply capacity, even with a lag. Indeed,
the post-meeting minutes highlighted the financial stability
risks if valuations in this area should disappoint “even if the
technology were ultimately successful”, as if the latter was not
yet clear. This language points to a generally downbeat view of

Australian supply capacity that also emerges in its discussion
of productivity trends. Although its position piece in the August
2025 Statement on Monetary Policy  indicated that lower trend
growth in productivity had no implications for inflation or
monetary policy, more recent statements imply that it does.
More broadly, the minutes for the September meeting revealed
a tendency to contemplate more hawkish interpretations
of data and outcomes. For example, the minutes dismissed

evidence of the labour market easing a bit more than
was forecast by pointing to the changes to the survey
arrangements. At the same time, the minutes contemplated
that the rate of unemployment consistent with inflation
being at target might have risen, even though wages growth
has marginally undershot the RBA’s forecasts and the cost
pressures cited by its business liaison contacts are mostly
non-labour costs. The minutes also included an extended

discussion of the possibility that the global neutral interest
rate might have increased recently. If true, this would imply
that financial conditions were less restrictive. This conclusion
would be hard to square with yield curves that have flattened
since the beginning of the year and other central bank
estimates of the term premium having increased, both of which
suggest tighter financial conditions.
That said, the RBA will find it hard to look past inflation

outcomes in the second half of year that is likely to remain
sticky. We anticipate trimmed mean inflation to record
quarterly outcomes of around 0.9%qtr in Q3 and Q4, remaining
around the current 3½%yr pulse. The Monetary Policy Board
will want to see clear evidence of inflation coming down before
withdrawing policy restrictiveness, so we continue to pencil in
August as the likely date for start of the rate-cutting phase.
